**Los Angeles** (often shortened as **LA** or **L.A.**)[^cite_note-16] is the [most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_municipalities_in_California) in the U.S. state of [California](5407.md), and the commercial, [financial](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_District,_Los_Angeles), and [cultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Los_Angeles) center of [Southern California](62520.md). With an estimated 3.87 million residents within the city limits as of 2025,[^cite_note-QuickFacts-8] it is the [second-most populous city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_cities_by_population) in the United States, behind [New York City](645042.md), and the largest city in the [Western United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States). The city has an [ethnically](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_groups_in_Los_Angeles) and culturally diverse population, and is the principal city of a [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_statistical_areas) of 12.9 million people (2024). [Greater Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles), a [combined statistical area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Combined_statistical_area) that includes the Los Angeles and [Riverside–San Bernardino metropolitan areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inland_Empire), is a sprawling [metropolis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolis) of over 18 million residents.

The majority of the city proper lies in the [Los Angeles Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Basin) adjacent to the Pacific Ocean in the west and extending partly through the [Santa Monica Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_Mountains) and north into the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley), with the city bordering the [San Gabriel Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gabriel_Valley) to its east. It covers about 469 square miles (1,210 km<sup>2</sup>),[^cite_note-CenPopGazetteer2021-6] and is the [county seat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_seat) and most populated city of [Los Angeles County](79734.md), which is the [most populous county in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_the_most_populous_counties_in_the_United_States) with an estimated 9.86 million residents as of 2022.[^cite_note-LosAngelesCountyDecline2022-17] It is the [third-most visited city in the U.S.](577095.md) (after New York City and [Miami](53846.md)), with over 3.6 million visitors as of 2023.[^cite_note-18]

The area that became Los Angeles was originally inhabited by the [indigenous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_people_of_California) [Tongva people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongva) and later claimed by [Juan Rodríguez Cabrillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Cabrillo) for [Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) in 1542. The city was founded on September 4, 1781, under Spanish governor [Felipe de Neve](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Felipe_de_Neve), on the village of [Yaanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaanga).[^cite_note-\:02-19] It became a part of the [First Mexican Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Empire) in 1821 following the [Mexican War of Independence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_War_of_Independence). In 1848, at the end of the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War), Los Angeles and the rest of California were purchased as part of the [Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe_Hidalgo) and became part of the United States. Los Angeles was [incorporated](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Municipal_corporation) as a municipality on April 4, 1850, five months before California achieved [statehood](18618239.md). The discovery of [oil](23195.md) in the 1890s brought rapid growth to the city.[^cite_note-20] The city was further expanded with the completion of the [Los Angeles Aqueduct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Aqueduct) in 1913, which delivers water from [Eastern California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eastern_California).

Los Angeles has a diverse economy with a broad range of industries. Despite a decline in film production, it is the oldest and one of the largest hubs of film and television production.[^cite_note-LAFilmProductionDeclining-21] It has [one of the busiest container ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles) in the Americas.[^cite_note-22][^cite_note-23][^cite_note-24] Despite an exodus of commerce and an [increasingly unreliable water supply](15380061.md), Downtown Los Angeles is evolving as a cultural center with the world's largest showcase of architecture designed by [Frank Gehry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Gehry).[^cite_note-DowntownLosAngelesHurting-25] In 2024, the [Los Angeles metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles) had a [gross metropolitan product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product) of over \$1.295 trillion,[^cite_note-fred-26] making it the city with the [third-largest GDP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_GDP) in the world, after [New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area) and [Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tokyo_metropolitan_area). Los Angeles hosted the [Summer Olympics](27566.md) in [1932](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics) and [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics), and will host the games for a third time in [2028](12695048.md).

## Toponymy

See also: [Etymology of place names in Los Angeles County, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Etymology_of_place_names_in_Los_Angeles_County,_California)

On September 4, 1781, a group of 44 settlers known as "[Los Pobladores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Pobladores)" founded the pueblo (town) they called [El Pueblo de Nuestra Señora la Reina de los Ángeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pueblo_de_Nuestra_Se%C3%B1ora_la_Reina_de_los_%C3%81ngeles), 'The Town of [Our Lady the Queen of the Angels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Our_Lady_the_Queen_of_the_Angels?action=edit\&redlink=1)'.[^cite_note-almanac-27] The original name of the settlement is disputed; the [Guinness Book of World Records](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guinness_Book_of_World_Records) rendered it as "El Pueblo de Nuestra Señora la Reina de los Ángeles del Río [Porciúncula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Portiuncula)"; other sources have shortened or alternate versions of the longer name.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29]

The local English pronunciation of the name of the city has varied over time. A 1953 article in the [journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Names_\(journal\)) of the [American Name Society](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Name_Society) asserts that the pronunciation [/lɔːs ˈændʒələs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*lawss AN-jəl-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key) was established following the 1850 incorporation of the city and that since the 1880s the pronunciation [/loʊs ˈæŋɡələs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) [*lohss ANG-gəl-əs*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:Pronunciation_respelling_key) emerged from a trend in California to give places Spanish (or Spanish-sounding) names and pronunciations.[^cite_note-stein-30] In 1908, librarian [Charles Fletcher Lummis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Fletcher_Lummis), who argued for the name's pronunciation with a hard *g* ([/ɡ/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English)),[^cite_note-31][^cite_note-32] reported that there were at least 12 pronunciation variants.[^cite_note-33] In the early 1900s, the *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* advocated for pronouncing it *Loce AHNG-hayl-ais* ([/loʊs ˈɑːŋheɪleɪs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English)), approximating Spanish [\[los ˈaŋxeles\]](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/Spanish), by printing the [respelling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Respelling) under its masthead for several years.[^cite_note-harvey-34] This did not find favor.[^cite_note-35]

Since the 1930s, [/lɔːs ˈændʒələs/](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Help\:IPA/English) has been most common.[^cite_note-36] In 1934, the [United States Board on Geographic Names](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Board_on_Geographic_Names) decreed that this pronunciation be used by the federal government.[^cite_note-harvey-34] This was also endorsed in 1952 by a "jury" appointed by Mayor [Fletcher Bowron](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fletcher_Bowron) to devise an official pronunciation.[^cite_note-stein-30][^cite_note-harvey-34]

## History

Main article: [History of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Los_Angeles)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Los_Angeles).

For a topical guide, see [Outline of the history of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Outline_of_the_history_of_Los_Angeles).

### Indigenous history

The settlement of [Indigenous Californians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_California) in the modern [Los Angeles Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Basin) and the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley) was dominated by the [Tongva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongva) (now also known as the *Gabrieleño* since the era of Spanish colonization). The historic center of Tongva power in the region was the settlement of [Yaanga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yaanga) ([Tongva](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tongva_language): *Iyáangẚ*), meaning "place of the [poison oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toxicodendron_diversilobum)", which would eventually become the site where the Spanish founded the [Pueblo de Los Ángeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pueblo_de_Los_%C3%81ngeles). *Iyáangẚ* has also been translated as "the valley of smoke".[^cite_note-37][^cite_note-38][^cite_note-google86-39][^cite_note-sfgate2002-40][^cite_note-\:02-19]

### Spanish rule

In 1542, maritime explorer [Juan Rodríguez Cabrillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Rodr%C3%ADguez_Cabrillo) claimed the present-day area of southern California for the [Spanish Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Empire) during an official military expedition northward along the [Pacific](23070.md) coast from [New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain).[^cite_note-41] [Gaspar de Portolà](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaspar_de_Portol%C3%A0) and [Franciscan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Franciscan) missionary [Juan Crespí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juan_Cresp%C3%AD) reached the present site of Los Angeles on August 2, 1769.[^cite_note-42]



The [Spanish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Spain) founded [Mission San Fernando Rey de España](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_San_Fernando_Rey_de_Espa%C3%B1a) in 1797.

In 1771, Franciscan [friar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Friar) [Junípero Serra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jun%C3%ADpero_Serra) directed the building of the [Mission San Gabriel Arcángel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_San_Gabriel_Arc%C3%A1ngel), the first [mission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_\(station\)) in the area.[^cite_note-LeffingwellWorden2005-43] On September 4, 1781, a group of 44 settlers known as "[Los Pobladores](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Pobladores)" founded the pueblo (town) they called [El Pueblo de Nuestra Señora la Reina de los Ángeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pueblo_de_Nuestra_Se%C3%B1ora_la_Reina_de_los_%C3%81ngeles), 'The Town of Our Lady the Queen of the Angels'.[^cite_note-almanac-27] The present-day city has the largest [Roman Catholic archdiocese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Archdiocese_of_Los_Angeles) in the United States. Two-thirds of the Mexican or ([New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain)) settlers were [mestizo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mestizo) or [mulatto](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mulatto), a mixture of African, indigenous, and European ancestry.[^cite_note-44] There was even one Asian Filipino from [Manila](184334.md) (Antonio Miranda Rodríguez) who was reassigned to nearby Santa Barbara which was a fortress that needed his gun-smithing skills more.[^cite_note-45] The settlement remained a small ranch town for decades, but by 1820, the population had increased to about 650 residents.[^cite_note-46] Today, the pueblo is commemorated in the historic district of [Los Angeles Pueblo Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Pueblo_de_Los_%C3%81ngeles_Historical_Monument) and [Olvera Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olvera_Street), the oldest part of Los Angeles.[^cite_note-Estrada2006-47]

### Mexican rule



[Californio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Californio) statesman [Pío Pico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%ADo_Pico), who served as the last [Mexican governor of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_governors_of_California_before_1850), played an influential role in the development of Los Angeles in the late Mexican and early American eras.

[New Spain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Spain) achieved its independence from the Spanish Empire in 1821, after which the pueblo existed within the [Mexican Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Mexican_Republic). During Mexican rule, Governor [Pío Pico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C3%ADo_Pico) made Los Angeles the regional capital of [Alta California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alta_California).[^cite_note-48] By this time, the new republic introduced more [secularization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_secularization_act_of_1833) acts within the Los Angeles region.[^cite_note-49] In 1846, during the [Mexican–American War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican%E2%80%93American_War), marines from the United States occupied the pueblo. This resulted in the [siege of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Siege_of_Los_Angeles), where 150 Mexican militias fought the occupiers, who eventually surrendered.[^cite_note-50]

Mexican rule ended following the American [Conquest of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conquest_of_California), part of the larger Mexican-American War. Americans took control from the [Californios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Californios) after a series of battles, culminating with the signing of the [Treaty of Cahuenga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Cahuenga) on January 13, 1847.[^cite_note-51] The [Mexican Cession](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_Cession) was formalized in the [Treaty of Guadalupe Hidalgo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Guadalupe_Hidalgo) in 1848, which ceded Los Angeles and the rest of Alta California to the United States.

### Post-Conquest era

See also: [Victorian Downtown Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victorian_Downtown_Los_Angeles) and [Los Angeles in the 1920s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_in_the_1920s)



The [Treaty of Cahuenga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Cahuenga), signed in 1847 by Californio [Andrés Pico](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andr%C3%A9s_Pico) and American [John C. Frémont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_C._Fr%C3%A9mont), ended the U.S. [Conquest of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conquest_of_California).

[Railroads arrived](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Los_Angeles#Railroads) with the completion of the [Southern Pacific](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Pacific_Railroad) line from [San Francisco](49728.md) to Los Angeles in 1876 and the [Santa Fe Railroad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atchison,_Topeka_and_Santa_Fe_Railway) in 1885.[^cite_note-Mulholland2002-52] Petroleum was discovered in the city and surrounding area in 1892, and by 1923, the discoveries helped [California](5407.md) become the country's largest oil producer, accounting for about one-quarter of the world's petroleum output.[^cite_note-Kipen2011-53]

By 1900, the population had grown to more than 102,000,[^cite_note-54] putting pressure on the city's [water supply](684928.md).[^cite_note-55] The completion of the [Los Angeles Aqueduct](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Aqueduct) in 1913, under the supervision of [William Mulholland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_Mulholland), ensured the continued growth of the city.[^cite_note-Reisner1993-56] Because of clauses in the city's charter that prevented the City of Los Angeles from selling or providing water from the aqueduct to any area outside its borders, many adjacent cities and communities felt compelled to join Los Angeles.[^cite_note-LAgrowth-57][^cite_note-58][^cite_note-FinalReport-59]



In the early 20th century, [Hollywood studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_film_studios), like [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures), helped transform [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles) into the world capital of film and helped solidify LA as a global economic hub.

Los Angeles created the first municipal [zoning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoning) ordinance in the United States. On September 14, 1908, the [Los Angeles City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Council,_1889%E2%80%931909) promulgated residential and industrial land use zones. The new ordinance established three residential zones of a single type, where industrial uses were prohibited. The proscriptions included barns, lumber yards, and any industrial land use employing machine-powered equipment. These laws were enforced against industrial properties after the fact. These prohibitions were in addition to existing activities that were already regulated as nuisances. These included explosives warehousing, gas works, oil drilling, slaughterhouses, and [tanneries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tannery). Although the council designated seven industrial zones, exemptions granted between 1908 and 1915 permitted industrial development within designated residential areas. There are two differences between the 1908 Residence District Ordinance and later zoning laws in the United States. First, the 1908 laws did not establish a comprehensive zoning map as the [1916 New York City Zoning Ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoning_in_the_United_States#Origins_and_history) did. Second, the residential zones did not distinguish types of housing; they treated apartments, hotels, and detached single-family housing equally.[^cite_note-weiss80-60]

In 1910, Hollywood merged into Los Angeles, with 10 movie companies already operating in the city at the time. By 1921, more than 80 percent of the world's film industry was concentrated in L.A.[^cite_note-Buntin2010-61] The money generated by the industry kept the city insulated from much of the economic loss suffered by the rest of the country during the [Great Depression](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Depression_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-YoungYoung2007-62]  By 1930, the population surpassed one million.[^cite_note-63] In 1932, the city hosted the [Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics).

### Post–World War II



The [California Shipbuilding Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Shipbuilding_Corporation) on [Terminal Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_Island) was among the many builders that made the [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles) one of the country's largest [shipyards](213654.md) during World War II.

During the [Second World War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_home_front_during_World_War_II), Los Angeles became a major center of wartime manufacturing, such as shipbuilding and aircraft. [Calship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Shipbuilding_Corporation) built hundreds of [Liberty Ships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberty_Ship) and [Victory Ships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victory_Ship) on Terminal Island, and the Los Angeles area was the headquarters of six of the country's major aircraft manufacturers ([Douglas Aircraft Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Douglas_Aircraft_Company), [Hughes Aircraft](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hughes_Aircraft_Company), [Lockheed](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lockheed_Corporation), [North American Aviation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Aviation), [Northrop Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northrop_Corporation), and [Vultee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vultee_Aircraft)). During the war, more aircraft were produced in one year than in all the pre-war years since the Wright brothers flew their first airplane in 1903, combined. Manufacturing in Los Angeles skyrocketed, and as [William S. Knudsen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/William_S._Knudsen), of the National Defense Advisory Commission, put it, "We won because we smothered the enemy in an avalanche of production, the like of which he had never seen, nor dreamed possible."[^cite_note-64]

Following [World War II](32927.md), a major influx of new residents and the expansion of defense and aerospace industries accelerated Los Angeles' urbanization. The resulting metropolitan growth caused the city to expand rapidly, [sprawling](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_sprawl) northwest into the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley).[^cite_note-Bruegmann2006-65] The expansion of the state owned [Interstate Highway System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System) during the 1950s and 1960s helped propel suburban growth and signaled the demise of the city's privately owned [electrified rail system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Electric), once the world's largest.

As a consequence of World War II, suburban growth, and population density, many amusement parks were built and operated in this area.[^cite_note-Braun-66] An example is [Beverly Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Park_\(amusement_park\)), which was located at the corner of Beverly Boulevard and La Cienega before being closed and replaced by the [Beverly Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Center).[^cite_note-Jennings-67]

In the late 20th century, Los Angeles substantially reduced the amount of housing that could be built by drastically [downzoning](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoning_in_the_United_States) the city. In 1960, the city had a total zoned capacity for approximately 10 million people. By 1990, that capacity had fallen to 4.5 million as a result of policy decisions to ban housing through zoning.[^cite_note-68]

Racial tensions led to the [Watts riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watts_riots) in 1965, resulting in 34 deaths and over 1,000 injuries.[^cite_note-Hinton2-69]



Opening ceremony of the [1984 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics) at the [LA Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum)

In 1969, California became the birthplace of the Internet, as the first [ARPANET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ARPANET) transmission was sent from the [University of California, Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_California,_Los_Angeles) (UCLA) to the [Stanford Research Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanford_Research_Institute) in [Menlo Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Menlo_Park,_California).[^cite_note-HafnerLyon1999-70]

In 1973, [Tom Bradley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tom_Bradley_\(American_politician\)) was elected as the city's first African American mayor, serving for five terms until retiring in 1993. Other events in the city during the 1970s included the [Symbionese Liberation Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbionese_Liberation_Army)'s [South Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Los_Angeles) standoff in 1974 and the [Hillside Stranglers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hillside_Stranglers) murder cases in 1977–1978.[^cite_note-vronsky187-71]

In early 1984, the city surpassed [Chicago](6886.md) in population to become the second-largest city in the United States.[^cite_note-72]

In 1984, the city hosted the Summer Olympic Games [for the second time](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics). Despite being [boycotted by 14 Communist countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics_boycott), the 1984 Olympics became more financially successful than any previous,[^cite_note-73] and the second Olympics to turn a profit; the other, according to an analysis of contemporary newspaper reports, was the [1932 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics), also held in Los Angeles.[^cite_note-Zarnowski-74]



[Wilshire Grand Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilshire_Grand_Center), built in 2017, is the [tallest building in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_California) and in the [Western United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States).

Racial tensions erupted on April 29, 1992, with the acquittal by a [Simi Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Simi_Valley) jury of four [Los Angeles Police Department](218985.md) (LAPD) officers captured on videotape beating [Rodney King](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rodney_King), culminating in [large-scale riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_riots_of_1992).[^cite_note-RuckerUpton2007-75][^cite_note-76]

In 1994, the magnitude 6.7 [Northridge earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northridge_earthquake) shook the city, causing \$12.5 billion in damage and 72 deaths.[^cite_note-77] The century ended with the [Rampart scandal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rampart_scandal), one of the most extensive documented cases of police misconduct in American history.[^cite_note-78]

### 21st century

In 2002, Mayor [James Hahn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/James_Hahn) led the campaign against secession, resulting in voters defeating efforts by the San Fernando Valley and Hollywood to secede from the city.[^cite_note-79]

In [2022](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2022_Los_Angeles_mayoral_election), [Karen Bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Bass) became the city's first female [mayor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Los_Angeles), making Los Angeles the largest U.S. city to have ever had a woman as mayor.[^cite_note-80]

In January 2025, a series of [devastating wildfires](78831062.md) caused by severe winds swept through Southern California, with the [Pacific Palisades fire](78829321.md) causing widespread destruction in Los Angeles's northwestern community of [Pacific Palisades](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Palisades,_Los_Angeles), with many calling it the most destructive in the history of the city of Los Angeles.[^cite_note-81]

In June 2025, the city experienced [protests and riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_2025_Los_Angeles_protests) following raids by [United States Immigration and Customs Enforcement](216537.md) (ICE).[^cite_note-82] The Trump administration has targeted Los Angeles over its sanctuary city status, [sending in hundreds of federal agents](80838047.md) to apprehend undocumented immigrants.[^cite_note-83] Trump also dispatched the Army National Guard and US Marines without the consent of local governments.[^cite_note-84]

Los Angeles will host the [2028 Summer Olympics](12695048.md) and [Paralympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2028_Summer_Paralympics), making Los Angeles the third city to host the Olympics three times, after [London](17867.md) and [Paris](22989.md).[^cite_note-85][^cite_note-86]

## Geography

See also: [Los Angeles Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Basin) and [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley)

### Topography



Satellite view of Los Angeles

The city of Los Angeles covers a total area of 502.7 square miles (1,302 km<sup>2</sup>), comprising 468.7 square miles (1,214 km<sup>2</sup>) of land and 34.0 square miles (88 km<sup>2</sup>) of water.[^cite_note-gazetteer-87] The city extends for 44 miles (71 km) from north to south and for 29 miles (47 km) from east to west. The perimeter of the city is 342 miles (550 km).

Los Angeles is both flat and hilly. The highest point in the city proper is [Mount Lukens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Lukens) at 5,074 ft (1,547 m),[^cite_note-88][^cite_note-89] located in the foothills of the [San Gabriel Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gabriel_Mountains) at the north extent of the [Crescenta Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crescenta_Valley). The eastern end of the [Santa Monica Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_Mountains) stretches from [Downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles) to the Pacific Ocean and separates the Los Angeles Basin from the San Fernando Valley. Other hilly parts of Los Angeles include the [Mt. Washington](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mt._Washington,_Los_Angeles) area north of Downtown, eastern parts such as [Boyle Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyle_Heights), the [Crenshaw district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crenshaw,_Los_Angeles) around the [Baldwin Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baldwin_Hills,_Los_Angeles), and the [San Pedro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Pedro,_Los_Angeles) district.

Surrounding the city are much higher mountains. Immediately to the north lie the [San Gabriel Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gabriel_Mountains), a popular recreation area for Angelenos. Its high point is [Mount San Antonio](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_San_Antonio), locally known as Mount Baldy, which reaches 10,064 feet (3,068 m). Further afield, the highest point in southern California is [San Gorgonio Mountain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gorgonio_Mountain), 81 miles (130 km) east of downtown Los Angeles,[^cite_note-90] with a height of 11,503 feet (3,506 m).

The [Los Angeles River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_River), which is largely seasonal, is the primary [drainage channel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drainage_channel). It was straightened and lined in 51 miles (82 km) of concrete by the [Army Corps of Engineers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Army_Corps_of_Engineers) to act as a flood control channel.[^cite_note-Gumprecht2001-91] The river begins in the [Canoga Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canoga_Park,_Los_Angeles) district of the city, flows east from the San Fernando Valley along the north edge of the Santa Monica Mountains, and turns south through the city center, flowing to its mouth in the Port of [Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Beach,_California) at the Pacific Ocean. The smaller [Ballona Creek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ballona_Creek) flows into the [Santa Monica Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_Bay) at [Playa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playa_del_Rey,_Los_Angeles).

### Vegetation

See also: [California coastal sage and chaparral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_coastal_sage_and_chaparral)



[Del Rey Lagoon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Del_Rey_Lagoon_Park) in [Playa del Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Playa_del_Rey)

Los Angeles is rich in native plant species partly because of its diversity of habitats, including beaches, [wetlands](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wetland), and mountains. The most prevalent plant communities are [coastal sage scrub](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coastal_sage_scrub), [chaparral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chaparral) shrubland, and [riparian woodland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Riparian_woodland).[^cite_note-Miller2008-92] Native plants include the [California poppy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_poppy), [matilija poppy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matilija_poppy), [toyon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heteromeles), [ceanothus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ceanothus), [chamise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chamise), [coast live oak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coast_live_oak), [sycamore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Platanus_racemosa), [willow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Willow) and [giant wildrye](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leymus_condensatus). Many of these native species, such as the [Los Angeles sunflower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helianthus_nuttallii), have become so rare as to be considered endangered. [Mexican fan palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washingtonia_filifera_var._robusta), [Canary Island palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phoenix_canariensis), [queen palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Syagrus_romanzoffiana), [date palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Date_palm), and [California fan palms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Washingtonia_filifera) are common in the Los Angeles area, although only the last is native to California, though still not native to the City of Los Angeles.

Los Angeles has several official flora:

- The official tree of Los Angeles is the coral tree (*[Erythrina afra](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Erythrina_afra)*).

  [^cite_note-Innovation1979-93]

- The official flower is the bird of paradise (*[Strelitzia reginae](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strelitzia_reginae)*).

  [^cite_note-Communications2003-94]

- The official plant is toyon (*[Heteromeles arbutifolia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heteromeles)*).

  [^cite_note-95]

### Fauna

See also: [List of wild animals from Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_wild_animals_from_Los_Angeles)

The city has an urban [population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_genetics) of [bobcats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bobcat) (*Lynx rufus*).[^cite_note-Serieys-et-al-2014-bundle-96] [Mange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mange) is a common problem in this population.[^cite_note-Serieys-et-al-2014-bundle-96] Although Serieys *et al.* 2014 find [selection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Selection_\(biology\)) of [immune](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immunity_\(biology\)) genetics at several [loci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gene_locus) they do not demonstrate that [this produces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adaptation) a [real difference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Phenotype) which helps the bobcats to [survive future mange outbreaks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disease_resistance).[^cite_note-Serieys-et-al-2014-bundle-96]

Bats, coyotes, [raccoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Raccoon), [opossums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Virginia_opossum), [skunks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Striped_skunk), snakes, and squirrels are wild animals commonly found in the Los Angeles area.[^cite_note-97][^cite_note-98] [Mountain lions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cougar), [mule deer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mule_deer), and other wild animals are also commonly spotted in Southern California.[^cite_note-99]

### Geology



[Mount Lukens](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mount_Lukens), in the [San Gabriel Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gabriel_Mountains), is the highest point in LA.

Los Angeles is subject to earthquakes because of its location on the [Pacific Ring of Fire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ring_of_Fire). The geologic instability has produced numerous [faults](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fault_\(geology\)), which cause approximately 10,000 earthquakes annually in Southern California, though most of them are too small to be felt.[^cite_note-quakes-100] The [strike-slip](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Strike-slip_fault) [San Andreas Fault](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Andreas_Fault) system, which sits at the boundary between the [Pacific Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Plate) and the [North American Plate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_American_Plate), passes through the Los Angeles metropolitan area. The segment of the fault passing through Southern California experiences a major earthquake roughly every 110 to 140 years, and [seismologists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seismologist) have warned about the next "big one", as the last major earthquake was the [1857 Fort Tejon earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1857_Fort_Tejon_earthquake).[^cite_note-101] The Los Angeles basin and metropolitan area are also at risk from [blind thrust earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blind_thrust_earthquake).[^cite_note-102] Major earthquakes that have hit the Los Angeles area include the [1933 Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1933_Long_Beach_earthquake), [1971 San Fernando](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1971_San_Fernando_earthquake), [1987 Whittier Narrows](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1987_Whittier_Narrows_earthquake), and the [1994 Northridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Northridge_earthquake) events. All but a few are of low intensity and are not felt. The USGS has released the [UCERF California earthquake forecast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_earthquake_forecast), which models earthquake occurrence in California. Parts of the city are also vulnerable to [tsunamis](31161.md); harbor areas were damaged by waves from [Aleutian Islands earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1946_Aleutian_Islands_earthquake) in 1946, [Valdivia earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1960_Valdivia_earthquake) in 1960, [Alaska earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1964_Alaska_earthquake) in 1964, [Chile earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Chile_earthquake) in 2010 and [Japan earthquake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2011_T%C5%8Dhoku_earthquake_and_tsunami) in 2011.[^cite_note-103]

### Cityscape

Main article: [List of districts and neighborhoods in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_districts_and_neighborhoods_in_Los_Angeles)

See also: [List of tallest buildings in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Los_Angeles)

The city is divided into many different districts and neighborhoods,[^cite_note-104][^cite_note-105] some of which had been separately incorporated cities that eventually merged with Los Angeles.[^cite_note-Abu-Lughod1999-106] These neighborhoods were developed piecemeal, and are well-defined enough that the city has signage which marks nearly all of them.[^cite_note-107]

#### Overview



View of the [downtown Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles) skyline from [Griffith Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffith_Park)

The city's street patterns generally follow a [grid plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grid_plan), with uniform block lengths and occasional roads that cut across blocks. However, this is complicated by rugged terrain, which has necessitated having different grids for each of the valleys that Los Angeles covers. Major streets are designed to move large volumes of traffic through many parts of the city, many of which are extremely long; [Sepulveda Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sepulveda_Boulevard) is 43 miles (69 km) long, while [Foothill Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foothill_Boulevard_\(Southern_California\)) is over 60 miles (97 km) long, reaching as far east as San Bernardino. Drivers in Los Angeles suffer from one of the worst rush hour periods in the world, according to an annual traffic index by navigation system maker, [TomTom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TomTom). LA drivers spend an additional 92 hours in traffic each year. During the peak rush hour, there is 80% congestion, according to the index.[^cite_note-108]

Los Angeles is often characterized by the presence of [low-rise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low-rise) buildings, in contrast to New York City. Outside of a few centers such as [downtown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles), [Warner Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Center), [Century City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Century_City), [Koreatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreatown,_Los_Angeles), [Miracle Mile](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miracle_Mile,_Los_Angeles), [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles), and [Westwood](374055.md), skyscrapers and high-rise buildings are not common in Los Angeles. However, downtown Los Angeles alone has many buildings over 30 stories, with fourteen over 50 stories, and two over 70 stories, the tallest of which is the [Wilshire Grand Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilshire_Grand_Center).

### Climate

Main article: [Climate of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Climate_of_Los_Angeles)

| Los Angeles (Downtown) |
| --- |
| Climate chart ([explanation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Climate_chart/How_to_read_a_climate_chart)) |
| J F M A M J J A S O N D        3.3     68 49       3.6     68 50       2.2     70 52       0.7     72 55       0.3     74 58       0.1     77 61       0     82 65       0     84 65       0.1     83 64       0.6     79 60       0.8     73 53       2.5     67 48   █ Average max. and min. temperatures in °F █ Precipitation totals in inchesSource: NOAA[^cite_note-Los_Angeles_Downtown_Weatherbox_NOAA_txt-109] |
| Metric conversion  J F M A M J J A S O N D        84     20 9       92     20 10       57     21 11       18     22 13       8.1     23 15       2.3     25 16       0.5     28 18       0     29 19       3.3     28 18       15     26 16       20     23 12       63     20 9   █ Average max. and min. temperatures in °C █ Precipitation totals in mm |

Los Angeles has a two-season [semi-arid climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Semi-arid_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification): *BSh*) with dry summers and mild to warm winters, but it receives more annual precipitation than most semi-arid climates, narrowly missing the boundary of a Mediterranean climate (Köppen: *Csb* on the coast, *Csa* otherwise).[^cite_note-Peel-110] Daytime temperatures are generally temperate all year round. In winter, they average around 68 °F (20 °C).[^cite_note-111] Autumn months tend to be hot, with major heat waves a common occurrence in September and October, while the spring months tend to be cooler and experience more precipitation. Los Angeles has plenty of sunshine throughout the year, with an average of only 35 days with measurable precipitation annually.[^cite_note-weatherbase-112]

Temperatures in the coastal basin exceed 90 °F (32 °C) on a dozen or so days in the year, from one day a month in April, May, June and November to three days a month in July, August, October and to five days in September.[^cite_note-weatherbase-112] Temperatures in the San Fernando and San Gabriel Valleys are considerably warmer. Temperatures are subject to substantial daily swings; in inland areas, the difference between the average daily low and the average daily high is over 30 °F (17 °C).[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113] The average annual temperature of the sea is 63 °F (17 °C), from 58 °F (14 °C) in January to 68 °F (20 °C) in August.[^cite_note-114] Hours of sunshine total more than 3,000 per year, from an average of 7 hours of sunshine per day in December to an average of 12 in July.[^cite_note-115]

Due to the mountainous terrain of the surrounding region, the Los Angeles area contains a large number of distinct [microclimates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Microclimate), causing extreme variations in temperature in close physical proximity to each other. For example, the average July maximum temperature at the [Santa Monica Pier](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_Pier) is 70 °F (21 °C) whereas it is 95 °F (35 °C) in Canoga Park, 15 miles (24 km) away.[^cite_note-climateofCA-116] The city, like much of the Southern Californian coast, is subject to a late spring/early summer weather phenomenon called "[June Gloom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/June_Gloom)". This involves overcast or foggy skies in the morning that yield to sun by early afternoon.[^cite_note-Poole2010-117]



[Lake Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Reservoir) in the [Santa Monica Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Monica_Mountains)

More recently, statewide [droughts in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Droughts_in_California) have further strained the city's [water security](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Water_security).[^cite_note-drought_continues-118] Downtown Los Angeles averages 14.67 in (373 mm) of precipitation annually, mainly occurring between November and March,[^cite_note-119][^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113] generally in the form of moderate rain showers, but sometimes as heavy rainfall during winter storms. Rainfall is usually higher in the hills and coastal slopes of the mountains because of [orographic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orographic) uplift. Summer days are usually rainless. Rarely, an incursion of moist air from the south or east can bring brief thunderstorms in late summer, especially to the mountains. The coast gets slightly less rainfall, while the inland and mountain areas get considerably more. Years of average rainfall are rare. The usual pattern is a year-to-year variability, with a short string of dry years of 5–10 in (130–250 mm) rainfall, followed by one or two wet years with more than 20 in (510 mm).[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113] Wet years are usually associated with warm water [El Niño](317311.md) conditions in the Pacific, dry years with cooler water [La Niña](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Ni%C3%B1a) episodes. A series of rainy days can bring floods to the lowlands and mudslides to the hills, especially after [wildfires](56106.md) have denuded the slopes.



[Venice Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice,_Los_Angeles) on the [South Coast of California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Coast_\(California\))

Both freezing temperatures and snowfall are extremely rare in the city basin and along the coast, with the last occurrence of a 32 °F (0 °C) reading at the downtown station being January 29, 1979;[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113] freezing temperatures occur nearly every year in valley locations, while the mountains within city limits typically receive snowfall every winter. The greatest snowfall recorded in downtown Los Angeles was 2.0 inches (5 cm) on January 15, 1932.[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113][^cite_note-BurtStroud2007-120] While the most recent snowfall occurred in February 2019, the first snowfall since 1962,[^cite_note-121][^cite_note-122] with snow falling in areas adjacent to Los Angeles as recently as January 2021.[^cite_note-123] Brief, localized instances of hail can occur on rare occasions, but are more common than snowfall. At the official downtown station, the highest recorded temperature is 113 °F (45 °C) on September 27, 2010,[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113][^cite_note-RecordHighTemp113-124] while the lowest is 28 °F (−2 °C),[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113] on January 4, 1949.[^cite_note-NCDC-CANOGA-113] Within the City of Los Angeles, the highest temperature ever officially recorded is 121 °F (49 °C), on September 6, 2020, at the weather station at [Pierce College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Pierce_College) in the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley) neighborhood of [Woodland Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Woodland_Hills,_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-125] During autumn and winter, [Santa Ana winds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Ana_winds) sometimes bring much warmer and drier conditions to Los Angeles, and raise wildfire risk.

### Environmental issues

Further information: [Pollution in California § Los Angeles air pollution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollution_in_California#Los_Angeles_air_pollution)

| External audio |
| --- |
| audio icon ["Fighting Smog in Los Angeles"](https://www.sciencehistory.org/distillations/podcast/fighting-smog-in-los-angeles), *Distillations* Podcast, 2018 [Science History Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science_History_Institute) |

Owing to geography, heavy reliance on automobiles, and the Los Angeles/Long Beach port complex, Los Angeles suffers from air pollution in the form of smog. The [Los Angeles Basin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Basin) and the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley) are susceptible to [atmospheric inversion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inversion_\(meteorology\)), which holds in the exhaust from road vehicles, airplanes, locomotives, shipping, manufacturing, and other sources.[^cite_note-134]



Smog in Los Angeles in December 2005

The smog season lasts from approximately May to October.[^cite_note-Information1983-135] While other large cities rely on rain to clear smog, Los Angeles gets only 15 inches (380 mm) of rain each year: pollution accumulates over many consecutive days. Issues of air quality in Los Angeles and other major cities led to the passage of early national environmental legislation, including the [Clean Air Act](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Clean_Air_Act). When the act was passed, California was unable to create a [State Implementation Plan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Implementation_Plan) that would enable it to meet the new air quality standards, largely because of the level of pollution in Los Angeles generated by older vehicles.[^cite_note-136] More recently, the state of California has led the nation in working to limit pollution by mandating [low-emission vehicles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Low-emission_vehicle). Smog is expected to continue to drop in the coming years because of aggressive steps to reduce it, which include [electric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_car) and [hybrid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_electric_vehicle) cars, improvements in [mass transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_transit), and other measures.

The number of Stage 1 smog alerts in Los Angeles has declined from over 100 per year in the 1970s to almost zero in the new millennium.[^cite_note-137] Despite improvement, the 2006 and 2007 annual reports of the [American Lung Association](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Lung_Association) ranked the city as the most polluted in the country with short-term particle pollution and year-round particle pollution.[^cite_note-shortTermPollutedCities-138] In 2008, the city was ranked the second most polluted and again had the highest year-round particulate pollution.[^cite_note-139] The city met its goal of providing 20 percent of the city's power from renewable sources in 2010.[^cite_note-Renewable_Energy-140] The American Lung Association's 2013 survey ranks the metro area as having the nation's worst smog, and fourth in both short-term and year-round pollution amounts.[^cite_note-141]

Los Angeles is also home to the nation's largest urban [oil field](2910801.md). There are more than 700 active oil wells within 1,500 feet (460 m) of homes, churches, schools, and hospitals in the city, a situation about which the [EPA](58666.md) has voiced serious concerns.[^cite_note-142]

### Total population, age, and sex

The [2020 U.S. census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2020_United_States_census) reported Los Angeles had a population of 3,898,747.[^cite_note-h138-143] The population density was 8,304.2 people per square mile (3,168 people per square kilometer). 5.2% of the total population is under 5 years old, 19.5% under 18 and 13.8% 65 years old and over.[^cite_note-h138-143] Females make up 50.2% of the total population.[^cite_note-h138-143] The city has lost 0.5% of its population since the 2020 census. The latest official estimate (2025) from the U.S. Census Bureau showed the population of Los Angeles County shrinking fastest among large U.S. counties nationwide.[^cite_note-LosAngelesHautingPopulationLoss-160]

### Housing and families

Owner-occupied housing units make up 36.3% of the total Los Angeles housing units, and they cost \$879,500 on average. (2019–2023)[^cite_note-h138-143] With a mortgage, the median selected monthly owner costs are \$3,399, and without a mortgage \$950. (2019–2023)[^cite_note-h138-143] Median gross rent is \$1,879. (2019–2023)[^cite_note-h138-143] There are 1,419,663 households in Los Angeles, with an average of 2.64 people being part of them. (2019–2023).[^cite_note-h138-143]

### Languages

Well over 200 languages are spoken in Los Angeles. The 2021 [American Community Survey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Community_Survey) of the [U.S. Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Census_Bureau) reported that some 56.8% of city residents aged five and older spoke a language other than English at home.[^cite_note-161]

### Economy



Percentage of households with incomes above \$150k across [Los Angeles County](79734.md) census tracts

Around 66.5% of the total population aged 16 and over make up Los Angeles in civilian labor force, while among female residents aged 16 and over the percentage is 61.0%.[^cite_note-h138-143] In 2022, accommodation and food services made \$17,366,966, health care and social assistance sectors made \$46,297,839, transportation and warehousing \$25,410,257, and the retail sector \$81,351,523, with residents spending an average of \$21,281 in retail purchases throughout the year.[^cite_note-h138-143] From 2019 to 2023, the median household income in Los Angeles was \$80,366 (2023 dollars), while the per capita income in the past 12 months was \$46,270.[^cite_note-h138-143] About 16.5% of Los Angeles residents have total incomes below the federal poverty line.[^cite_note-h138-143]

### Race and ethnicity



Los Angeles [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Los_Angeles)

According to data in 2023 from the [United States Census Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_Census_Bureau), Los Angeles's population is 47.2% [Hispanic or Latino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic_and_Latino_Americans), 28.3% [non-Hispanic White](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Non-Hispanic_whites), 8.5% [Black](2154.md), 12.0% [Asian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asian_Americans), 1.2% [Native American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Americans_in_the_United_States) and 0.1% [Pacific Islander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Islander_Americans).[^cite_note-162] [Ethnic enclaves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethnic_enclave) like [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Los_Angeles), [Historic Filipinotown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Filipinotown,_Los_Angeles), [Koreatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreatown,_Los_Angeles), [Little Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Armenia,_Los_Angeles), [Little Ethiopia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Ethiopia,_Los_Angeles), [Tehrangeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehrangeles), [Little Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Tokyo,_Los_Angeles), [Little Bangladesh](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Bangladesh,_Los_Angeles), and [Thai Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_Town,_Los_Angeles) provide examples of the [multicultural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multiculturalism) character of Los Angeles. People of [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_American) ancestry formed the largest national origin group, comprising 31.9% of the city's population, followed by those of [Salvadoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvadoran_American) (6.0%) and [Guatemalan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guatemalan_American) (3.6%) heritage. Descendants of Mexicans and Central Americans have long-established communities in Los Angeles and are spread throughout the entire metropolitan area. They are most heavily concentrated in regions around Downtown, such as [East Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Los_Angeles_\(region\)), [Northeast Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Los_Angeles) and [Westlake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westlake,_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-163]

The largest Asian ethnic groups are [Filipinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipino_American) (3.2%) and [Koreans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean_American) (2.9%), which have their own established ethnic enclaves—[Koreatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreatown,_Los_Angeles) in the Wilshire Center and [Historic Filipinotown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Filipinotown).[^cite_note-164] [Chinese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_American) people, which make up 1.8% of Los Angeles's population, reside mostly outside of Los Angeles city limits, in the [San Gabriel Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Gabriel_Valley) of eastern Los Angeles County, but make a sizable presence in the city, notably in [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown,_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-165] Chinatown and [Thaitown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thaitown,_Los_Angeles,_California) are also home to many [Thais](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_American) and [Cambodians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cambodian_American), which make up 0.3% and 0.1% of Los Angeles's population, respectively. The [Japanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_American) comprise 0.9% of the city's population and have an established [Little Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Tokyo,_Los_Angeles) in the city's downtown, and another significant community of Japanese Americans is in the [Sawtelle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sawtelle,_Los_Angeles) district of West Los Angeles. [Indians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indian_American) make up 0.9% of the city's population. [Vietnamese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vietnamese_American) make up 0.5% of Los Angeles's population.

Los Angeles is also home to Caucasian and Middle Eastern communities, such as [Armenians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armenian_American), [Assyrians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assyrian_Americans), and [Iranians](2234641.md), many of whom live in enclaves like [Little Armenia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Armenia,_Los_Angeles) and [Tehrangeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tehrangeles).[^cite_note-166][^cite_note-167]

Due to [racial segregation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Racial_segregation_in_the_United_States) that ended in the late 20th century, African Americans have been the predominant ethnic group in [South Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Los_Angeles), which has emerged as the largest African-American community in the western United States since the 1960s. The neighborhoods of South Los Angeles with highest concentration of African Americans include [Crenshaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crenshaw,_Los_Angeles), [Baldwin Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baldwin_Hills,_Los_Angeles), [Leimert Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leimert_Park,_Los_Angeles), [Hyde Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hyde_Park,_Los_Angeles), [Gramercy Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gramercy_Park,_Los_Angeles), [Manchester Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manchester_Square,_Los_Angeles) and [Watts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watts,_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-MaryEllen-168] Since the 1990s, the growing cost of living in the city has significantly impacted the African American population. African Americans are the fastest declining population in the city, and many of the formerly predominantly African American neighborhoods have become much more diverse.[^cite_note-169][^cite_note-170] There is also a sizable [Eritrean](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eritrean_Americans) and [Ethiopian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ethiopian_Americans) community in the Fairfax region.[^cite_note-171]

Los Angeles has the second-largest Mexican, Armenian, Salvadoran, Filipino, and Guatemalan populations by city in the world, the third-largest Canadian population in the world, and has the largest Japanese, Iranian/Persian, Cambodian, and Romani (Gypsy) populations in the country.[^cite_note-172] The Italian community is concentrated in San Pedro.[^cite_note-173]

Most of Los Angeles's foreign-born population were born in [Mexico](3966054.md), [El Salvador](9356.md), [Guatemala](17238567.md), the [Philippines](23440.md) and [South Korea](27019.md).[^cite_note-174]

### Religion

|  |  |  |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Christian |  | 65% |
| [Catholic](606848.md) |  | 32% |
| [Protestant](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestant) |  | 30% |
| Other Christian |  | 3% |
| Unaffiliated |  | 25% |
| Jewish |  | 3% |
| [Muslim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Muslim) |  | 2% |
| [Buddhist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Buddhist) |  | 2% |
| [Hindu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Los_Angeles) |  | 1% |
| Other faiths |  | 1% |

According to a 2014 study by the [Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center), [Christianity](5211.md) is the most prevalently practiced religion in Los Angeles (65%).[^cite_note-LAReligion1-175][^cite_note-LAReligion2-176] The [Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Los_Angeles) is the largest [archdiocese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diocese) in the country.[^cite_note-177] [Cardinal Roger Mahony](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roger_Mahony), as the archbishop, oversaw construction of the [Cathedral of Our Lady of the Angels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Angels), which opened in September 2002 in downtown Los Angeles.[^cite_note-178]

In 2011, the once common, but ultimately lapsed, custom of conducting a procession and [Mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_\(liturgy\)) in honor of Nuestra Señora de los Ángeles, in commemoration of the founding of the City of Los Angeles in 1781, was revived by the [Queen of Angels Foundation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Queen_of_Angels_Foundation) and its founder Mark Albert, with the support of the Archdiocese of Los Angeles as well as several civic leaders.[^cite_note-179] The recently revived custom is a continuation of the original processions and Masses that commenced on the first anniversary of the founding of Los Angeles in 1782 and continued for nearly a century thereafter.



[Cathedral of Our Lady of the Angels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Angels) (also called Los Angeles Cathedral), the metropolitan cathedral of the [Roman Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Church) in Los Angeles

With 621,000 [Jews](1131183.md) in the metropolitan area, the region has the second-largest population of Jews in the United States, after [New York City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judaism_in_New_York_City).[^cite_note-Jews-180] Many of Los Angeles's Jews now live on the [Westside](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Westside_\(Los_Angeles_County\)) and in the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley), though [Boyle Heights](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boyle_Heights,_Los_Angeles,_California) once had a large Jewish population before World War II due to [restrictive housing covenants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Covenant_\(law\)#Exclusionary_covenants). Major Orthodox Jewish neighborhoods include [Hancock Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hancock_Park,_Los_Angeles), [Pico-Robertson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pico-Robertson), and [Valley Village](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Valley_Village,_Los_Angeles), while Jewish Israelis are well represented in the [Encino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Encino,_Los_Angeles) and [Tarzana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tarzana,_Los_Angeles) neighborhoods, and [Persian Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Persian_Jews) in [Beverly Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beverly_Hills,_California). Many varieties of Judaism are represented in the greater Los Angeles area, including [Reform](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_Judaism), [Conservative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservative_Judaism), [Orthodox](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orthodox_Judaism), and [Reconstructionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reconstructionist_Judaism). The [Breed Street Shul](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Breed_Street_Shul) in [East Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Los_Angeles,_California), built in 1923, was the largest synagogue west of Chicago in its early decades; it is no longer in daily use as a synagogue and is being converted to a museum and community center.[^cite_note-181][^cite_note-182] The [Kabbalah Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kabbalah_Centre) also has a presence in the city.[^cite_note-183]

The [International Church of the Foursquare Gospel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Church_of_the_Foursquare_Gospel) was founded in Los Angeles by [Aimee Semple McPherson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aimee_Semple_McPherson) in 1923 and remains headquartered there to this day. For many years, the church convened at [Angelus Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angelus_Temple), which, at its construction, was one of the largest churches in the country.[^cite_note-184]



[Wilshire Boulevard Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilshire_Boulevard_Temple) is one of the largest [synagogues](48559.md) in L.A.

Los Angeles has had a rich and influential Protestant tradition. The first Protestant service in Los Angeles was a Methodist meeting held in a private home in 1850, and the oldest Protestant church still operating, [First Congregational Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Congregational_Church_of_Los_Angeles), was founded in 1867.[^cite_note-Eighteen5019Thirty-185] In the early 1900s the [Bible Institute Of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible_Institute_Of_Los_Angeles) published the founding documents of the [Christian Fundamentalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christian_Fundamentalist) movement and the [Azusa Street Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Azusa_Street_Revival) launched [Pentecostalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostalism).[^cite_note-Eighteen5019Thirty-185] The [Metropolitan Community Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_Community_Church) also had its origins in the Los Angeles area.[^cite_note-186] Important churches in the city include [First Presbyterian Church of Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Presbyterian_Church_of_Hollywood), [Bel Air Presbyterian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bel_Air_Presbyterian_Church), [First African Methodist Episcopal Church of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_African_Methodist_Episcopal_Church_of_Los_Angeles), [West Angeles Church of God in Christ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Angeles_Church_of_God_in_Christ), [Second Baptist Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Baptist_Church_\(Los_Angeles,_California\)), [Crenshaw Christian Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crenshaw_Christian_Center), [McCarty Memorial Christian Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/McCarty_Memorial_Christian_Church), and First Congregational Church.



[Second Church of Christ, Scientist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Church_of_Christ,_Scientist_\(Los_Angeles\))

The Hollywood region of Los Angeles also has several significant headquarters and churches, including the [Celebrity Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Celebrity_Center) of the [Church of Scientology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Church_of_Scientology).[^cite_note-187]

Because of Los Angeles's large multi-ethnic population, a wide variety of faiths are practiced, including [Buddhism](3267529.md), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism_in_Los_Angeles), [Islam](6037917.md), [Zoroastrianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zoroastrianism), [Sikhism](27964.md), [Baháʼí](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith), various [Eastern Orthodox Churches](10186.md), [Sufism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sufism), [Shintoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shinto), [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism), [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism), [Chinese folk religion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_folk_religion) and countless others. Immigrants from Asia, for example, have formed several significant Buddhist congregations, making the city home to the greatest variety of Buddhists in the world. The first Buddhist [joss house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_temple_architecture) was founded in the city in 1875.[^cite_note-Eighteen5019Thirty-185] [Atheism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Atheism) and other secular beliefs are also common, as the city is the largest in the Western U.S. [Unchurched Belt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unchurched_Belt).

### Homelessness

Main article: [Homelessness in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_Los_Angeles)



Homeless tents outside [Los Angeles City Hall](1221425.md), 2021

As of January 2024, there are 45,252 [homeless people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homelessness_in_the_United_States) in the City of Los Angeles, comprising roughly 60% of the homeless population of LA County.[^cite_note-188] This is a 2.2% decrease from the previous year (with a 0.3% decrease in the overall homeless population of LA County).[^cite_note-\:0-189][^cite_note-Homeless_Populations-190] The epicenter of homelessness in Los Angeles is the [Skid Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skid_Row,_Los_Angeles) neighborhood, which contains 8,000 homeless people, one of the largest stable populations of homeless people in the United States.[^cite_note-191][^cite_note-abc7_06132019-192] The increased homeless population in Los Angeles has been attributed to lack of housing affordability[^cite_note-Homeless_Populations-190] and to substance abuse.[^cite_note-193] Almost 60 percent of the 82,955 people who became newly homeless in 2019 said their homelessness was because of economic hardship.[^cite_note-\:0-189] In Los Angeles, black people are roughly four times more likely to experience homelessness.[^cite_note-\:0-189][^cite_note-194]

## Economy

Further information: [Los Angeles Chamber of Commerce](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Chamber_of_Commerce)

See also: [Los Angeles County, California § Economy](79734.md#Economy)



Employment by industry in [Los Angeles County](79734.md) in 2015

The economy of Los Angeles is driven by international trade, entertainment (television, motion pictures, video games, music recording, and production), aerospace, technology, petroleum, fashion, apparel, and tourism. Other significant industries include finance, telecommunications, law, healthcare, and [transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-195] In the 2017 [Global Financial Centres Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index), Los Angeles was ranked the 19th most competitive financial center in the world and sixth most competitive in the U.S. after [New York City](3848717.md#Wall_Street), [San Francisco](49728.md#Economy), [Chicago](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Chicago#Finance), [Boston](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Boston), and [Washington, D.C.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Washington,_D.C.)[^cite_note-196] Although many businesses have left [downtown Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles) following the [COVID-19 pandemic](62750956.md), efforts are underway to re-invent the neighborhood as a cultural center with a large architectural showcase in [Bunker Hill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bunker_Hill,_Los_Angeles) designed by [Frank Gehry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frank_Gehry).[^cite_note-DowntownLosAngelesHurting-25]

Of the five [major film studios](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_film_studio), only [Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures) is within Los Angeles's city limits;[^cite_note-197] it is located in the so-called [Thirty-Mile Zone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Studio_zone) of entertainment headquarters in Southern California. Their parent company [Paramount Skydance Corporation](79917576.md) has had its corporate headquarters in Los Angeles since 2025.[^cite_note-HQ-198]

Los Angeles is still the largest manufacturing center in the United States by employment, but now second after the [New York metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area) by dollar value of goods manufactured. Disadvantages of manufacturing production in Los Angeles are its relative geographical isolation from most major North American markets, lack of a reliable water supply, and lack of an adequate skilled labor force, in addition to a significantly complex regulatory environment.[^cite_note-199] The contiguous [ports of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles) and [Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Long_Beach) together comprise the busiest port in the United States by some measures and the fifth busiest port in the world, vital to trade within the [Pacific Rim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Rim).[^cite_note-200]



The combined [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles)-[Port of Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Long_Beach) is the fifth-busiest port in the world.

The [Los Angeles metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_metropolitan_area) has a [gross metropolitan product](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product) of over \$1.0 trillion (as of 2018),[^cite_note-bea-201] making it the third-largest economic metropolitan area in the world, after [New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_York_metropolitan_area) and [Tokyo](354951.md).[^cite_note-bea-201] Los Angeles has been classified an "[alpha world city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city)" according to a 2012 study by a group at [Loughborough University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loughborough_University).[^cite_note-202]

As of 2018, Los Angeles is home to three [Fortune 500](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortune_500) companies: [AECOM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/AECOM), [CBRE Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBRE_Group), and [Reliance Steel & Aluminum Co](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reliance_Steel_&_Aluminum_Co.).[^cite_note-Fortune_500-203] Other companies headquartered in Los Angeles and the surrounding metropolitan area include [The Aerospace Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Aerospace_Corporation), [California Pizza Kitchen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Pizza_Kitchen),[^cite_note-204] [Capital Group Companies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capital_Group_Companies), [Deluxe Entertainment Services Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deluxe_Entertainment_Services_Group), [Dine Brands Global](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dine_Brands_Global), [DreamWorks Animation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/DreamWorks_Animation), [Dollar Shave Club](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dollar_Shave_Club), [Fandango Media](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fandango_Media), [Farmers Insurance Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmers_Insurance_Group), [Forever 21](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Forever_21), [Hulu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hulu), [Panda Express](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Panda_Express), [SpaceX](832774.md), [Ubisoft Film & Television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ubisoft_Film_&_Television), [The Walt Disney Company](37398.md), [Universal Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_Pictures), [Warner Bros.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Bros.), [Warner Music Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Music_Group), and [Trader Joe's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trader_Joe's).

At the end of the second quarter of 2024, Los Angeles saw an office space vacancy rate of 31.5%, a 33.5% increase year-over-year[^cite_note-auto-205][^cite_note-\:1-206] Retail vacancy stood at 8.6%, a 15% increase year-over-year.[^cite_note-\:1-206]



[Downtown Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Downtown_Los_Angeles) is the [central business district](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_business_district) of the city.
