## Arts and culture

Main article: [Culture of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Los_Angeles)



The city's historic center at [Plaza de Los Ángeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Plaza) near [Calle Olvera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olvera_Street)

Los Angeles is often billed as the creative capital of the world because one in every six of its residents works in a creative industry[^cite_note-208] and there are more artists, writers, filmmakers, actors, dancers and musicians living and working in Los Angeles than any other city at any other time in [world history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_history).[^cite_note-Only_In_LA-209] Los Angeles is strongly influenced by Mexican American culture due to California formerly being part of Mexico and, previously, the Spanish Empire.[^cite_note-210] The city is also known for [its prolific murals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murals_of_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-shatkin2013-211]

### Landmarks

See also: [List of sites of interest in the Los Angeles area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sites_of_interest_in_the_Los_Angeles_area) and [National Register of Historic Places listings in Los Angeles, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Register_of_Historic_Places_listings_in_Los_Angeles,_California)



[El Cabrillo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/El_Cabrillo), a [Spanish Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Colonial_Revival) style [National Historic Landmark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Historic_Landmark)

The [architecture of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Los_Angeles#Architecture) is influenced by its Spanish, Mexican, and American roots. Popular styles in the city include [Spanish Colonial Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spanish_Colonial_Revival) style, [Mission Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mission_Revival) style, [California Churrigueresque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Churrigueresque) style, [Mediterranean Revival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mediterranean_Revival) style, [Art Deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Deco_architecture) style, and [Mid-Century Modern](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mid-century_modern) style, among others.

Important landmarks in Los Angeles include the [Hollywood Sign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Sign),[^cite_note-212] [Walt Disney Concert Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Concert_Hall), [Capitol Records Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Capitol_Records_Building),[^cite_note-213] the [Cathedral of Our Lady of the Angels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cathedral_of_Our_Lady_of_the_Angels),[^cite_note-214] [Angels Flight](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angels_Flight),[^cite_note-215] [Grauman's Chinese Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grauman's_Chinese_Theatre),[^cite_note-216] [Dolby Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolby_Theatre),[^cite_note-217] [Griffith Observatory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Griffith_Observatory),[^cite_note-218] [Getty Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Getty_Center),[^cite_note-219] [Getty Villa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Getty_Villa),[^cite_note-220] [Stahl House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stahl_House),[^cite_note-221] the [Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum), [L.A. Live](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L.A._Live),[^cite_note-222] the [Los Angeles County Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Museum_of_Art), the [Venice Canal Historic District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venice_Canal_Historic_District) and boardwalk, [Theme Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Theme_Building), [Bradbury Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bradbury_Building), [U.S. Bank Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Bank_Tower), [Wilshire Grand Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilshire_Grand_Center), [Hollywood Boulevard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Boulevard), [Los Angeles City Hall](1221425.md), [Hollywood Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Bowl),[^cite_note-223] battleship [USS *Iowa*](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USS_Iowa_\(BB-61\)), [Watts Towers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Watts_Towers),[^cite_note-224] [Crypto.com Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto.com_Arena), [Dodger Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodger_Stadium), and [Olvera Street](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olvera_Street).[^cite_note-225]

### Movies and the performing arts

Further information: [Music of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Los_Angeles)

See also: [List of films set in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_set_in_Los_Angeles)



[Grauman's Chinese Theatre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grauman's_Chinese_Theatre) on the [Hollywood Walk of Fame](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Walk_of_Fame)

The performing arts play a major role in Los Angeles's cultural identity. According to the USC Stevens Institute for Innovation, "there are more than 1,100 annual theatrical productions and 21 openings every week."[^cite_note-Only_In_LA-209] The [Los Angeles Music Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Music_Center) is "one of the three largest performing arts centers in the nation", with more than 1.3 million visitors per year.[^cite_note-226] The [Walt Disney Concert Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Walt_Disney_Concert_Hall), centerpiece of the Music Center, is home to the prestigious [Los Angeles Philharmonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Philharmonic).[^cite_note-227] Notable organizations such as [Center Theatre Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Center_Theatre_Group), the [Los Angeles Master Chorale](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Master_Chorale), and the [Los Angeles Opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Opera) are also resident companies of the Music Center.[^cite_note-228][^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230] Talent is locally cultivated at premier institutions such as the [Colburn School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Colburn_School) and the [USC Thornton School of Music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USC_Thornton_School_of_Music).



[Hollywood Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Bowl) in [Hollywood Hills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Hills)

The city's [Hollywood neighborhood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles) has been recognized as the center of the [American motion picture industry](418334.md), having held this distinction since the early 20th century, and the Los Angeles area is also associated with being the center of the [U.S. television industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_the_United_States).[^cite_note-231] The city is home to major film studios as well as major record labels. Los Angeles plays host to the annual [Academy Awards](324.md), the [Primetime Emmy Awards](6579025.md), the [Grammy Awards](44635.md), as well as many other entertainment industry awards shows. Los Angeles is the site of the [USC School of Cinematic Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USC_School_of_Cinematic_Arts) which is the oldest film school in the United States.[^cite_note-232]

### Museums and galleries

See also: [List of museums in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Los_Angeles) and [List of museums in Los Angeles County, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_museums_in_Los_Angeles_County,_California)



The [Getty Villa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Getty_Villa) is one of the two campuses of the [J. Paul Getty Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Paul_Getty_Museum), alongside the [Getty Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Getty_Center).

There are 841 museums and art galleries in [Los Angeles County](79734.md), including many in the city of Los Angeles,[^cite_note-lmu-233] more museums per capita than any other city in the U.S.[^cite_note-lmu-233] Some of the notable museums are the [Los Angeles County Museum of Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Museum_of_Art) (the largest art museum in the Western United States[^cite_note-234]), the [Getty Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Getty_Center) (part of the [J. Paul Getty Trust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J._Paul_Getty_Trust), the world's wealthiest art institution[^cite_note-235]), the [Petersen Automotive Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Petersen_Automotive_Museum),[^cite_note-236] the [Huntington Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Huntington_Library),[^cite_note-237] the [Natural History Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natural_History_Museum_of_Los_Angeles_County),[^cite_note-238] the [Battleship Iowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battleship_Iowa),[^cite_note-239] [The Broad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Broad), which houses over 2,000 works of contemporary art[^cite_note-240] and the [Museum of Contemporary Art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Museum_of_Contemporary_Art,_Los_Angeles).[^cite_note-241] A significant number of art galleries are on [Gallery Row](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gallery_Row), and tens of thousands attend the monthly Downtown Art Walk there.[^cite_note-242]



Cactus Garden, Getty Center Los Angeles

### Libraries



[Los Angeles Central Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Public_Library)

The [Los Angeles Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Public_Library) system operates 72 public libraries in the city.[^cite_note-243] Enclaves of unincorporated areas are served by branches of the [County of Los Angeles Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/County_of_Los_Angeles_Public_Library), many of which are within walking distance to residents.[^cite_note-244]

### Cuisine

See also: [List of Michelin-starred restaurants in California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Michelin-starred_restaurants_in_California)

Los Angeles's food culture is a fusion of global cuisine brought on by the city's rich immigrant history and population. As of 2025, the [Michelin Guide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Michelin_Guide) recognized 20 starred restaurants including 2 restaurants [Providence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Providence_\(restaurant\)) and [Somni](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Somni) which earned 3 Michelin stars.[^cite_note-245]

Latin American immigrants, particularly [Mexican](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexican_cuisine) immigrants, brought [tacos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taco), [burritos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Burrito), [quesadillas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quesadilla), [tortas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Torta), [tamales](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamale), and [enchiladas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Enchilada) served from food trucks and stands, [taquerias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taqueria), and [cafés](370815.md). Asian restaurants, many immigrant-owned, exist throughout the city with hotspots in [Chinatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinatown),[^cite_note-246] [Koreatown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Koreatown,_Los_Angeles),[^cite_note-247] [Little Tokyo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Little_Tokyo,_Los_Angeles),[^cite_note-248] [Thai Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thai_Town,_Los_Angeles)[^cite_note-249] and [Historic Filipinotown](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historic_Filipinotown,_Los_Angeles)[^cite_note-250]. Los Angeles also carries an outsized offering of vegan, vegetarian, and plant-based options.

## Sports

Main article: [Sports in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sports_in_Los_Angeles)

See also: [Soccer in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soccer_in_Los_Angeles) and [History of the National Football League in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_National_Football_League_in_Los_Angeles)



[Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum)

Los Angeles and its metropolitan area are the home of twelve top-level professional sports teams, several of which play in neighboring communities but use Los Angeles in their name. These teams include the [Los Angeles Dodgers](18213.md)[^cite_note-251] and [Los Angeles Angels](1360083.md)[^cite_note-252] of [Major League Baseball](38776.md) (MLB), the [Los Angeles Rams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Rams)[^cite_note-253] and [Los Angeles Chargers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Chargers) of the [National Football League](21211.md) (NFL), the [Los Angeles Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Lakers)[^cite_note-254] and [Los Angeles Clippers](72892.md)[^cite_note-255] of the [National Basketball Association](22093.md) (NBA), the [Los Angeles Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Kings)[^cite_note-256] and [Anaheim Ducks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaheim_Ducks)[^cite_note-257] of the [National Hockey League](21809.md) (NHL), the [Los Angeles Galaxy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Galaxy)[^cite_note-258] and [Los Angeles FC](44255607.md)[^cite_note-259] of [Major League Soccer](71802.md) (MLS), the [Los Angeles Sparks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Sparks) of the [Women's National Basketball Association](57721.md) (WNBA),[^cite_note-260] and [Angel City FC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_City_FC) of the [National Women's Soccer League](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Women's_Soccer_League) (NWSL).[^cite_note-261] The region is also home to other professional teams including the [SoCal Lashings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SoCal_Lashings) of [Minor League Cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minor_League_Cricket) (MiLC) and the [Los Angeles Knight Riders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Knight_Riders) of [Major League Cricket](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Major_League_Cricket) (MLC).[^cite_note-262]

The city's top collegiate sports teams include the [UCLA Bruins](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UCLA_Bruins) and the [USC Trojans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/USC_Trojans) in the [National Collegiate Athletic Association](60706.md) (NCAA), both of which are Division I teams in the [Big Ten Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Ten_Conference).[^cite_note-263]



[Dodger Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodger_Stadium), home of the [Los Angeles Dodgers](18213.md) of [Major League Baseball](38776.md)

Los Angeles is the second-largest city in the United States but [hosted no NFL team between 1995 and 2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_National_Football_League_in_Los_Angeles). At one time, the Los Angeles area hosted two NFL teams: the [Rams](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Rams) and the [Raiders](22312.md). Both left the city in 1995, with the Rams moving to [St. Louis](27687.md), and the Raiders moving back to their original home of [Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland,_California). After 21 seasons in St. Louis, on January 12, 2016, the NFL announced the Rams would be moving back to Los Angeles for the [2016 NFL season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_NFL_season) with its home games played at the [Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum) for four seasons.[^cite_note-264][^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266] Before 1995, the Rams played their home games in the Coliseum from 1946 to 1979 which made them the first professional sports team to play in Los Angeles, and then moved to [Anaheim Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anaheim_Stadium) from 1980 until 1994. The [San Diego Chargers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Diego_Chargers) announced on January 12, 2017, that they would also relocate back to Los Angeles (the first since its inaugural season in 1960) and become the [Los Angeles Chargers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Chargers) beginning in the [2017 NFL season](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2017_NFL_season) and played at [Dignity Health Sports Park](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dignity_Health_Sports_Park) in [Carson, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carson,_California), for three seasons.[^cite_note-267] The Rams and the Chargers then moved to [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md) in [Inglewood](107640.md) during the 2020 season.[^cite_note-268]



[Crypto.com Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto.com_Arena), home to the [Los Angeles Lakers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Lakers), [Los Angeles Kings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Kings), and [Los Angeles Sparks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Sparks)

Los Angeles boasts a number of sports venues, including [Dodger Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dodger_Stadium),[^cite_note-269] the [Los Angeles Memorial Coliseum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Memorial_Coliseum),[^cite_note-270] [BMO Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMO_Stadium)[^cite_note-271] and [Crypto.com Arena](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crypto.com_Arena).[^cite_note-272] The [Kia Forum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kia_Forum), SoFi Stadium, Dignity Health Sports Park, the [Rose Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_\(stadium\)), [Angel Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Angel_Stadium), [Honda Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Honda_Center), and [Intuit Dome](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intuit_Dome) are also in adjacent cities and cities in Los Angeles's metropolitan area.[^cite_note-273]

Los Angeles has twice hosted the [Summer Olympic Games](27566.md): in [1932](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1932_Summer_Olympics) and in [1984](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1984_Summer_Olympics), and will host the games for a third time in [2028](12695048.md).[^cite_note-274] Los Angeles will be the third city after London ([1908](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1908_Summer_Olympics), [1948](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1948_Summer_Olympics) and [2012](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Summer_Olympics)) and Paris ([1900](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1900_Summer_Olympics), [1924](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1924_Summer_Olympics) and [2024](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2024_Summer_Olympics)) to host the Olympic Games three times. Los Angeles has [submitted 10 bids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_bids_for_the_Summer_Olympics) to host Olympic events to the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md), more than any other city in the world.[^cite_note-275] When the tenth Olympic Games were hosted in 1932, the former 10th Street was renamed Olympic Blvd. Los Angeles also hosted the [Deaflympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deaflympics) in [1985](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1985_Summer_Deaflympics)[^cite_note-276] and [Special Olympics World Summer Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_Olympics_World_Games) in [2015](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Special_Olympics_World_Summer_Games).[^cite_note-277]



[BMO Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/BMO_Stadium), home of [Los Angeles FC](44255607.md) of [Major League Soccer](71802.md)

Eight NFL [Super Bowls](27718.md) have also been held in the city and its surrounding areas – two at the Memorial Coliseum ([the first Super Bowl, I](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_I) and [VII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_VII)), five at the Rose Bowl in suburban [Pasadena](92408.md) ([XI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XI), [XIV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XIV), [XVII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XVII), [XXI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XXI), and [XXVII](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_XXVII)), and one at the suburban [Inglewood](107640.md) ([LVI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Super_Bowl_LVI)).[^cite_note-278] The Rose Bowl also hosts an annual and highly prestigious [NCAA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NCAA) [college football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/College_football) game called the [Rose Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_game), which happens every [New Year's Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Year's_Day).

Los Angeles hosted eight [FIFA World Cup](11370.md) soccer games at the [Rose Bowl](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rose_Bowl_\(stadium\)) in [1994](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup), including the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_FIFA_World_Cup_Final), where [Brazil](149286.md) defeated [Italy](362466.md) on penalty kicks. The Rose Bowl also hosted four matches during the [1999 FIFA Women's World Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup), including the [final](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1999_FIFA_Women's_World_Cup_Final), where the [United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_States_women's_national_soccer_team) defeated [China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_women's_national_football_team) on penalty kicks, after which [Brandi Chastain](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brandi_Chastain) took her shirt off after she scored the tournament-winning penalty kick. Los Angeles was one of eleven U.S. host cities for the [2026 FIFA World Cup](36581929.md), with eight matches being held at [SoFi Stadium](45525802.md).[^cite_note-279][^cite_note-280] The city was estimated to have hosted approximately 4.78 million supporters.[^cite_note-281]

Los Angeles is one of six North American cities to have won championships in all five of its major leagues (MLB, NFL, NHL, NBA, and MLS), having completed the feat with the Kings' [2012 Stanley Cup title](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2012_Stanley_Cup_Final).[^cite_note-282]

## Government

Main article: [Government of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Los_Angeles)

Further information: [List of elected officials in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_elected_officials_in_Los_Angeles)

See also: [Government of Los Angeles County](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Los_Angeles_County)



[Los Angeles City Hall](1221425.md), built in 1928, houses the [mayor of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Los_Angeles) and the [Los Angeles City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Council).

Los Angeles is a [charter city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charter_city) as opposed to a [general law city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_law_city). The current charter was adopted on June 8, 1999, and has been amended many times.[^cite_note-283] The [elected government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_elected_officials_in_Los_Angeles) consists of the [Los Angeles City Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Council) and the [mayor of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor_of_Los_Angeles), which operate under a [mayor–council government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mayor%E2%80%93council_government), as well as the [city attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Attorney) (not to be confused with the [district attorney](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_District_Attorney), a county office) and [controller](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_Controller). The mayor is [Karen Bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karen_Bass).[^cite_note-284] There are 15 city council districts.

The city has many departments and appointed officers, including the [Los Angeles Police Department](218985.md) (LAPD),[^cite_note-285] the [Los Angeles Board of Police Commissioners](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Board_of_Police_Commissioners),[^cite_note-286] the [Los Angeles Fire Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Fire_Department) (LAFD),[^cite_note-287] the [Housing Authority of the City of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_Authority_of_the_City_of_Los_Angeles) (HACLA),[^cite_note-288] the [Los Angeles Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Department_of_Transportation) (LADOT),[^cite_note-289] and the [Los Angeles Public Library](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Public_Library) (LAPL).[^cite_note-290]

The charter of the City of Los Angeles, ratified by voters in 1999, created a system of advisory neighborhood councils that would represent the diversity of stakeholders, defined as those who live, work, or own property in the neighborhood. The neighborhood councils are relatively autonomous and spontaneous in that they identify their own boundaries, establish their own bylaws, and elect their own officers. There are about 90 neighborhood councils.

Residents of Los Angeles elect [supervisors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_Board_of_Supervisors) for the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, and 4th supervisorial districts.

### Federal and state representation

In the [California State Assembly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Assembly), Los Angeles is split between fourteen districts.[^cite_note-291] In the [California State Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_State_Senate), the city is split between eight districts.[^cite_note-292] In the [United States House of Representatives](19468510.md), it is split among nine congressional districts.[^cite_note-293]

## Crime

Main article: [Crime in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Crime_in_Los_Angeles)

See also: [List of criminal gangs in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_criminal_gangs_in_Los_Angeles)



The [LAPD](218985.md) on [May Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/May_Day) 2006 in front of the new Caltrans District 7 Headquarters

In 1992, the city of Los Angeles recorded 1,092 murders.[^cite_note-294] Los Angeles experienced a significant decline in crime in the 1990s and late 2000s and reached a 50-year low in 2009 with 314 homicides.[^cite_note-295][^cite_note-296] This is a rate of 7.8 per 100,000—down from 1980, when it was 34.2.[^cite_note-297][^cite_note-298][^cite_note-299] In 2021, murders rose to their highest rate (8.5) since 2008, although by 2024 (6.1), the spike had receded.[^cite_note-300]

In 2015, it was revealed that the LAPD had been under-reporting a category of crime between 2005 and 2012, making the rate in the city appear lower than it was for that period.[^cite_note-301][^cite_note-302]

The [Dragna crime family](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_crime_family) and [Mickey Cohen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mickey_Cohen) dominated organized crime in the city during the [Prohibition era](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prohibition_in_the_United_States)[^cite_note-mafiaLA-303] and reached its peak during the 1940s and 1950s with the "Battle of Sunset Strip" as part of the [American Mafia](18293303.md), but has gradually declined since then with the rise of various black and [Hispanic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hispanic) gangs in the late 1960s and early 1970s.[^cite_note-mafiaLA-303]

According to the [Los Angeles Police Department](218985.md), the city is home to 45,000 gang members, organized into 450 gangs.[^cite_note-304] Among them are the [Crips](3434023.md) and [Bloods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bloods), which are both African American street gangs that originated in the [South Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Los_Angeles) region. Latino street gangs such as the [Sureños](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sure%C3%B1os), a Mexican American street gang, and [Mara Salvatrucha](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mara_Salvatrucha), which has mainly members of [Salvadoran](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Salvadoran_American) descent, as well as other [Central American](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_American) descendants, all originated in Los Angeles. This has led to the city being referred to as the "Gang Capital of America".[^cite_note-305]

- [Los Angeles City College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_City_College) (LACC)

  [^cite_note-329]

- [Los Angeles Harbor College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Harbor_College)

  [^cite_note-330]

- [Los Angeles Mission College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Mission_College)

  [^cite_note-331]

- [Los Angeles Pierce College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Pierce_College)

  [^cite_note-332]

- [Los Angeles Valley College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Valley_College) (LAVC)

  [^cite_note-333]

- [Los Angeles Southwest College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Southwest_College)

  [^cite_note-334]

- [Los Angeles Trade-Technical College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Trade-Technical_College)

  [^cite_note-335]

- [West Los Angeles College](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Los_Angeles_College)

  [^cite_note-336]

There are numerous additional colleges and universities outside the city limits in the Greater Los Angeles area, including the [Claremont Colleges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Claremont_Colleges) consortium, which includes the most selective liberal arts colleges in the U.S., and the [California Institute of Technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Institute_of_Technology) (Caltech), one of the top STEM-focused research institutions in the world.

### Schools

See also: [Los Angeles County, California § Education](79734.md#Education); and [List of high schools in Los Angeles County, California](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_high_schools_in_Los_Angeles_County,_California)

[Los Angeles Unified School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Unified_School_District) serves almost all of the city of Los Angeles, as well as several surrounding communities, with a student population of around 800,000.[^cite_note-337] After [Proposition 13](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/California_Proposition_13_\(1978\)) was approved in 1978, urban school districts had considerable trouble with funding. LAUSD has become known for its underfunded, overcrowded, and poorly maintained campuses, although its 162 Magnet schools help compete with local private schools.

Several small sections of Los Angeles are in the [Inglewood Unified School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inglewood_Unified_School_District),[^cite_note-338] and the [Las Virgenes Unified School District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Las_Virgenes_Unified_School_District).[^cite_note-339] The Los Angeles County Office of Education operates the [Los Angeles County High School for the Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_County_High_School_for_the_Arts).

## Media

Main article: [Media in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Los_Angeles)

See also: [List of television shows set in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_television_shows_set_in_Los_Angeles), [List of films set in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_films_set_in_Los_Angeles), and [List of Los Angeles television stations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Los_Angeles_television_stations)



The [Hollywood Sign](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Sign) is a prominent symbol of the [American film industry](418334.md).

The Los Angeles metro area is the second-largest broadcast [designated market area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Designated_market_area) in the U.S. (after [New York](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_New_York_City)) with 5,431,140 homes (4.956% of the U.S.), which is served by a wide variety of local [AM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Los_Angeles#AM) and [FM](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Los_Angeles#FM) radio and [television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Media_in_Los_Angeles#Television) stations. Los Angeles and New York City are the only two media markets to have seven [VHF](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/VHF) allocations assigned to them.[^cite_note-340]

The major daily English-language newspaper in the area is the *[Los Angeles Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)*.[^cite_note-341] *[La Opinión](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/La_Opini%C3%B3n)* is the city's major daily Spanish-language paper.[^cite_note-342] *[The Korea Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Korea_Times_\(Los_Angeles\))* is the city's major daily [Korean-language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Korean-language) paper while *[The World Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_World_Journal)* is the city and county's major Chinese newspaper. The *[Los Angeles Sentinel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Sentinel)* is the city's major African-American weekly paper, boasting the largest African-American readership in the Western United States.[^cite_note-343] *[Investor's Business Daily](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Investor's_Business_Daily)* is distributed from its LA corporate offices, which are headquartered in Playa del Rey.[^cite_note-344]



The former *[LA Times](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Times)* headquarters

As part of the region's aforementioned creative industry, the Big Five major broadcast television networks, [ABC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/American_Broadcasting_Company), [CBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CBS), [FOX](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fox_Broadcasting_Company), [NBC](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/NBC), and [The CW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_CW), all have production facilities and offices throughout various areas of Los Angeles. All four major broadcast television networks, plus major Spanish-language networks [Telemundo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telemundo) and [Univision](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Univision), also own and operate stations that both serve the Los Angeles market and serve as each network's West Coast [flagship station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flagship_station): ABC's [KABC-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KABC-TV) (Channel 7),[^cite_note-345] CBS's [KCBS-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCBS-TV) (Channel 2), Fox's [KTTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KTTV)-TV (Channel 11),[^cite_note-346] NBC's [KNBC](1246824.md)-TV (Channel 4),[^cite_note-347] The CW's [KTLA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KTLA)-TV (Channel 5), MyNetworkTV's [KCOP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCOP)-TV (Channel 13), Telemundo's [KVEA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KVEA)-TV (Channel 52), and Univision's [KMEX-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KMEX-DT) (Channel 34). The region also has four [PBS](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_Broadcasting_Service) member stations, with [KCET](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCET) re-joining the network as a secondary affiliate in August 2019, after spending the previous eight years as the nation's largest independent public television station. [KTBN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KTBN) (Channel 40) is the flagship station of the religious [Trinity Broadcasting Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trinity_Broadcasting_Network), based out of [Santa Ana](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Santa_Ana,_California). A variety of independent television stations, such as [KCAL-TV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/KCAL-TV) (Channel 9), also operate in the area.



[Paramount Pictures](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paramount_Pictures) Studios

There are also a number of smaller regional newspapers, alternative weeklies and magazines, including the *[Los Angeles Register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Freedom_Communications)*, Los Angeles Community News, (which focuses on coverage of the greater Los Angeles area), *[Los Angeles Daily News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Daily_News)* (which focuses coverage on the [San Fernando Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Fernando_Valley)), *[LA Weekly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LA_Weekly)*, *[L.A. Record](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/L.A._Record)* (which focuses coverage on the music scene in the [Greater Los Angeles Area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_Los_Angeles_Area)), *Los Angeles Magazine*, the *[Los Angeles Business Journal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Business_Journal)*, the *Los Angeles Daily Journal* (legal industry paper), *[The Hollywood Reporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hollywood_Reporter)*, *[Variety](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Variety_\(magazine\))* (both entertainment industry papers), and *[Los Angeles Downtown News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Downtown_News)*.[^cite_note-348] In addition to the major papers, numerous local periodicals serve immigrant communities in their native languages, including Armenian, English, Korean, Persian, Russian, Chinese, Japanese, Hebrew, and Arabic. Many cities adjacent to Los Angeles also have their own daily newspapers whose coverage and availability overlap with certain Los Angeles neighborhoods. Examples include *The [Daily Breeze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daily_Breeze)* (serving the [South Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Bay,_Los_Angeles)), and *The [Long Beach Press-Telegram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Beach_Press-Telegram)*.

Los Angeles arts, culture and nightlife news is also covered by a number of local and national online guides, including *Time Out Los Angeles*, *[Thrillist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Thrillist)*, *Kristin's List*, *DailyCandy*, *Diversity News Magazine*, *LAist*, and *Flavorpill*.[^cite_note-349][^cite_note-350][^cite_note-351][^cite_note-352]

## Infrastructure

### Transportation

Main article: [Transportation in Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transportation_in_Los_Angeles)

#### Freeways

Main article: [Southern California freeways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_California_freeways)



[Judge Harry Pregerson Interchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Judge_Harry_Pregerson_Interchange), connecting the [Century Freeway (I-105)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_105_\(California\)) and the [Harbor Freeway (I-110)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_110_and_State_Route_110_\(California\)) in [South LA](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Los_Angeles)

The city and the rest of the [Los Angeles metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_metropolitan_area) are served by an extensive network of freeways and highways. [Texas Transportation Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Texas_Transportation_Institute)'s annual Urban Mobility Report ranked Los Angeles area roads the most congested in the United States in 2019, as measured by annual delay per traveler, area residents experiencing a cumulative average of 119 hours waiting in traffic that year.[^cite_note-353] Los Angeles was followed by San Francisco/[Oakland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oakland), Washington, D.C., and [Miami](53846.md). Despite the congestion in the city, the mean daily travel time for commuters in Los Angeles is shorter than in other major cities, including New York City, [Philadelphia](50585.md), and Chicago. Los Angeles's mean travel time for work commutes in 2006 was 29.2 minutes, similar to those of San Francisco and Washington, D.C.[^cite_note-ACS2006-354][^cite_note-355]

The major highways that connect LA to the rest of the nation include [I-5](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_5_in_California), which runs south through [San Diego](28504.md) to the [Mexican border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mexico%E2%80%93United_States_border) and north through [Sacramento](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sacramento,_California), [Portland](23503.md), and [Seattle](11388236.md) to the [Canadian border](630260.md); [I-10](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_10_in_California), the southernmost east–west, coast-to-coast [interstate highway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interstate_Highway_System) in the United States, going to [Jacksonville](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacksonville,_Florida); and [US 101](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/U.S._Route_101_in_California), which heads to the [California Central Coast](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Coast_\(California\)), [San Francisco](49728.md), the [Redwood Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/North_Coast_\(California\)), and the [Oregon](26811621.md) and [Washington](13015878.md) coasts.

#### Public transit

Main article: [Los Angeles Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro)



[Los Angeles Metro Bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro_Bus) operated by the [Los Angeles Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro)

Bus service in Los Angeles is primarily operated by the [Los Angeles Metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro), which runs the second-largest [bus network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro_Bus) in the United States.[^cite_note-APTAdaily-356] The network provides extensive coverage in Los Angeles and surrounding municipalities. Metro operates two [bus rapid transit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bus_rapid_transit) lines, the [G](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/G_Line_\(Los_Angeles_Metro\)) and [J](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/J_Line_\(Los_Angeles_Metro\)) lines.[^cite_note-357][^cite_note-FactsGlance-358] Other bus providers include the [Los Angeles Department of Transportation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Department_of_Transportation), which contracts local and commuter bus services,[^cite_note-359] and municipal agencies such as [Big Blue Bus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Big_Blue_Bus) and [GTrans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GTrans).[^cite_note-360]

The Los Angeles Metro also operates the county's [subway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) and [light rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_rail) system. The [Los Angeles Metro Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro_Rail) consists of two subway lines and four light rail lines.[^cite_note-FactsGlance-358] As of 2025, the light rail system is the [busiest in the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_light_rail_systems_by_ridership), while the city's subway system is the [ninth busiest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_United_States_rapid_transit_systems_by_ridership).[^cite_note-APTAdaily-356] Since the opening of the first line, the [A Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/A_Line_\(Los_Angeles_Metro\)), in 1990, Metro Rail has been extended significantly, with further extensions underway.[^cite_note-PBS1-361][^cite_note-362] The system serves communities across Los Angeles County, with 110 stations and 125.3 mi (201.7 km) of track.[^cite_note-FactsGlance-358]



[Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Union_Station_\(Los_Angeles\)) is served by [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak), [Metrolink](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metrolink_\(California\)), and [Metro Rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Metro_Rail).

Los Angeles is the hub of Metrolink, the [commuter rail](6286.md) system serving [Southern California](62520.md), and is served by [Amtrak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amtrak) [inter-city rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inter-city_rail). The [Pacific Surfliner](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pacific_Surfliner) service, which operates between [San Diego](28504.md) and [San Luis Obispo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Luis_Obispo,_California) via Los Angeles,[^cite_note-363] is Amtrak's busiest line outside the [Northeast Corridor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Northeast_Corridor).[^cite_note-364] All Amtrak lines and Metrolink lines serving Los Angeles converge at [Union Station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_Union_Station), the city's primary rail terminal and regional transit hub.[^cite_note-365] Opened in 1939, it is the largest passenger rail terminal in the [Western United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Western_United_States), with over 1 million Amtrak boardings and alightings in 2025.[^cite_note-366][^cite_note-367]



LA Metro [B Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B_Line_\(Los_Angeles_Metro\)) train at Union Station

The primary payment method for the Los Angeles Metro and most other regional agencies is the [TAP card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TAP_card), a [contactless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contactless_smart_card) stored-value card.[^cite_note-368] According to the 2024 US Census Bureau American Community Survey, around 6.2% of working Los Angeles (city) residents commute to work via public transit.[^cite_note-369] Metro recorded approximately 951,500 weekday boardings in 2024, with buses accounting for more than two-thirds of ridership.[^cite_note-ridership-370]

#### Airports

Main article: [List of airports in the Los Angeles area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_the_Los_Angeles_area)

See also: [Los Angeles International Airport in popular culture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Los_Angeles_International_Airport_in_popular_culture)



[Los Angeles Intl. Airport](18131.md) (LAX) is the [eighth-busiest airport in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World's_busiest_airports_by_passenger_traffic).

The main international and domestic airport serving Los Angeles is [Los Angeles International Airport](18131.md), commonly referred to by its airport code, LAX.[^cite_note-371] It is located on the Westside of Los Angeles near the [Sofi Stadium](45525802.md) in Inglewood.

Other major nearby commercial airports include:

- [Ontario International Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ontario_International_Airport), owned by the city of Ontario, serves the Inland Empire.

  [^cite_note-372]

- [Hollywood Burbank Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood_Burbank_Airport), jointly owned by the cities of Burbank, Glendale, and Pasadena. Formerly known as Bob Hope Airport and Burbank Airport, it is the closest airport to downtown Los Angeles and serves the San Fernando, San Gabriel, and Antelope Valleys.

  [^cite_note-373]

- [Long Beach Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Beach_Airport) serves the Long Beach/Harbor area.

  [^cite_note-374]

- [John Wayne Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Wayne_Airport) of Orange County

One of the world's busiest general-aviation airports is also in Los Angeles: [Van Nuys Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Van_Nuys_Airport).[^cite_note-375]

#### Seaports



[Vincent Thomas Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Thomas_Bridge) at [Terminal Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Terminal_Island) in the [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles)

The [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles) is in [San Pedro Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Pedro_Bay_\(California\)) in the [San Pedro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/San_Pedro,_Los_Angeles) neighborhood, approximately 20 miles (32 km) south of downtown. Also called Los Angeles Harbor and WORLDPORT LA, the port complex occupies 7,500 acres (30 km<sup>2</sup>) of land and water along 43 miles (69 km) of waterfront. It adjoins the separate [Port of Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Long_Beach).[^cite_note-376]

The sea ports of the [Port of Los Angeles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Los_Angeles) and [Port of Long Beach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Long_Beach) together make up the *Los Angeles/Long Beach Harbor*.[^cite_note-377][^cite_note-378] Together, both ports are the fifth busiest [container](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Container_\(cargo\)) port in the world, with a trade volume of over 14.2 million [TEUs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twenty-foot_equivalent_unit) in 2008.[^cite_note-379] Singly, the Port of Los Angeles is the busiest container port in the United States and the largest cruise ship center on the [West Coast of the United States](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/West_Coast_of_the_United_States) – The Port of Los Angeles's World Cruise Center served about 590,000 passengers in 2014.[^cite_note-380]

There are also smaller, non-industrial harbors along Los Angeles's coastline. The port includes four bridges: the [Vincent Thomas Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vincent_Thomas_Bridge), [Henry Ford Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henry_Ford_Bridge), [Long Beach International Gateway Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Long_Beach_International_Gateway), and [Commodore Schuyler F. Heim Bridge](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commodore_Schuyler_F._Heim_Bridge). Passenger ferry service from San Pedro to the city of [Avalon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avalon,_California) (and [Two Harbors](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Two_Harbors,_California)) on [Santa Catalina Island](21076784.md) is provided by Catalina Express.
